                 2. A.     03.04.14 
2.       RECOGNITION 
A.  Spirit of Diversity Awards  
This evening we are pleased to recognize eleven groups and individuals 
for our Spirit of Diversity Awards. These awards recognize those who help 
foster a climate of inclusion and diversity that creates an optimal learning 
environment for the entire school community.  
This is the second year for the Spirit of Diversity Awards, which were 
designed by the Troy Diversity & Inclusion Council as a way to honor 
those who go above and beyond to ensure that every student is valued 
and diversity is celebrated within the Troy School District. We plan to 
continue these awards for years to come as a way to recognize the 
wonderful work that is done in our school community.  
We received many stellar nominations for these awards and would like to 
honor the following people. Please come forward as your name or the 
name of your organization is called. 
Spirit of Diversity Award 
Ruba Alexander, Nancy Hanoosh and Sue Younan—who are being 
recognized tonight for their work creating and sustaining the Giving Tree 
program at Troy Union, which works to help in the area of socio-economic 
diversity. The Giving Tree operates all year round and allows families with 
difficult finances to post items they need anonymously. Other families and 
teachers at school then purchase the items families have requested.  
The Baker Buddy Program—represented tonight by Kitty Schmidt, 
Laura Kemp and Kerry Daniels, as well as advisors Ping Bai, Kelly Chlebek, 
Zachary MacIntosh, Courtney Oudsema, and Suzy Powell and 8th grade 
mentors Mackenzie Bachert, Stephani Bell, Kloma Cardoza, Cade 
Chapman, Amy Bray-Cotton, Connor Fogo, Rachel Goodman, Evan Griffin,  
Arpitha Gowda, Deana Johnson, Tessa Kaminski, Nathan Li, Mick Nivens, 
Najieh Oweis, Dhara Patel, Liz Pop, Mary Sharaby, Evan Sketch, Sariah 
Stockdale and Katie Walter. The goal of the Baker Buddy program is to 
further develop a mentoring relationship between 6th grade students and 
3
8th grade students in which all students continue working on becoming an 
individual, standing up for others, and learning from others.    
Joe DeVault and the Boulan Park LINKS program. The LINKS 
Program, which Mr. DeVault developed, pairs 8th grade students with 
students in the Autism Spectrum Program to work on projects, activities, 
games and academic programs. The LINKS also accompany ASD students 
to their general education classes. The connections that occur with the 
ASD students and the LINKS is magical and a true link is formed for both 
groups of students. LINKS has truly changed the culture of Boulan Park 
Middle School. 
Lori Medina-Yashinski and Neil Yashinski—who are being recognized 
tonight for their efforts to promote the beautiful cultures that are 
represented at Leonard Elementary as well as to provide a warm 
environment to exchange experiences and grow in understanding of the 
diversity in our community. Through a parent group called Leonard 
Cultural Connections, Ms. Medina-Yashinki and Mr. Yashinski help families 
share their rich traditions and unique experiences with others.  
Stephanie Miller—who is being honored tonight for her work to create a 
culture of inclusion and appreciation as Principal of Morse Elementary. The 
parent who nominated Ms. Miller says “she fosters a climate where all 
kinds of diversity should be accepted, talked about and understood.  She 
applies this to not only differences in culture, but also in socioeconomic 
status, learning disability, and special needs (to name a few). She has 
created an environment where individuals can keep their identity and be 
part of the group as a whole.  Students and Staff are reminded by Ms. 
Miller that everyone is special in their own way and we should each 
embrace our differences and others differences to create an environment 
that we can all learn and coexist together.” 
Jesse Allgeier, Deb Sheldon, Hannah Nagi and Renee Jamil—who 
are being recognized tonight for their work on the Troy School District 
High School Consortium/Troy Social Justice Project. This group of 
educators volunteer to lead and facilitate a group of their high school 
students in a leading edge cadre of high school students from around the 
region, for the purpose of breaking stereotypes, cross regional boundaries 
often divided by race and class and come together throughout the school 
4
year with youth leaders  from other communities. They help students to 
participate in a series of four Youth Leadership Summits, each with a 
different theme and workshops focusing both on diversity & inclusion 
content as well as skills development. The difference they have made is 
tremendous. 
Honorable Mention 
Smith Middle School Science Teachers Tracey Kania and Duane 
Losey—who are being recognized for their work to ensure that every 
student can learn and thrive at Smith. They also strive to include lesson 
on cultural diversity within the science curriculum. 
Troy Union Teachers and Staff —who are being honored tonight for 
their efforts to ensure that every student is included and valued and 
embracing the diversity that makes up our community. 
Oakland ISD Social Justice Project and Dr. Jay Marks —who are 
being recognized tonight for their efforts to help our staff and students 
create the Troy Social Justice Project, as well as other programs 
supporting diversity in the Troy School District.  
Jen Hilzinger—who was also recognized last year but is being honored 
tonight for her many, many efforts in the area of diversity and inclusion in 
the Troy School District.  
RECOMMENDATION:  Distribute certificates.

B.  Troy Youth Assistance “Dollars for Education” 
Troy Youth Assistance (TYA) is a nonprofit organization committed to 
strengthening youth and families by preventing and reducing juvenile 
delinquency, child neglect and child abuse through community 
involvement.  This evening, we welcome Mr. Elliot Gold, a  representative 
from TYA, who has joined us to announce their annual fundraiser/raffle, 
“Dollars for Education.”  
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Mr. Elliot Gold will share details on the raffle.

Month,  in  recognition  of  the  outstanding  work  of 
building‐level  educational  leaders.  This  evening,  the 
Board will pass a resolution to commend elementary and

It  was  moved  by  Mrs.  Fleming  and  supported  by  Mrs. 
Edmunds that  
WHEREAS, energetic  and  inspiring  school  leadership  is 
essential  if  teachers,  schools  and  districts  are  to  ensure 
that all Troy students are college‐ and career‐ready; and, 
WHEREAS, K‐12  school  principals  play  a  vital  role  in  the 
success  of  students  and  act  as  the  liaison  between  the 
school  and  the  community  it  serves,  ensuring  that 
parents  and  the  community  are  aware  of  student  and 
school achievements; and, 
WHEREAS, Troy  School  District  principals  are 
dedicated, quality  educational  professionals  committed 
to providing sound leadership and guidance to their staff 
and students; and,  
WHEREAS, during  this  month,  we  join  with  educators, 
parents  and  students  throughout  Michigan  to  raise 
awareness  of  the  importance  of  educational  leadership, 
and to recognize and thank all principals working in Troy 
for their exemplary service; 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED that the Troy Board of 
Education  officially  recognizes  February  as  Principal 
Appreciation  Month  in  Troy  and  thanks  our  dedicated 
building leaders for all they do for our students and staff.

Principal Appreciation Month 
14‐015 
Throughout  the  State  of  Michigan,  February  has 
traditionally  been  celebrated  as  Principal  Appreciation 
from  Enviro‐Clean  Maintenance  Services ‐   John 
Thompson,  Director  of  Educational  Services  and  Craig 
Wyse,  Assistant  Director  of  Educational  Services.   They 
commitment to Troy Schools and the educational process 
by donating a check, in the amount of $5,000, to the Troy 
Foundation for Educational Excellence.   Mr. Al Robinson, 
president  of  TFEE,  was  in  attendance  to  represent  the 
Foundation and accept the donation.    
Student Representatives to the 
International Academy‐East, reported to the Board about 
one  of  the  clubs  offered  at  IA‐East  called  Model  United 
Nations.   The  club  lets  students  participate  in  a  mock 
United  Nations  debate  allowing  them  to  represent 
different viewpoints of different countries in order to find 
a  solution  to  them.   She  shared  that  20  students  would 
be  attending  a  Model  UN  Conference  in  Chicago  this 
week.   She also shared that student council is planning a 
school‐wide  scavenger  hunt  for  March.   She  also 
reported  that  IA‐East  has  decided  to  use  the  $500  prize 
money  from  Sodexo  to  purchase  flags  to  hang  in  the 
cafeteria  to  represent  the  different  countries  their 
students  come  from.   Finally,  she  invited  the  Board  and 
administration  to  the  IB  Art  Show,  which  will  take  place 
on April 16th.   
Michaela Smith, student representative from Athens High 
School,  reported  on  their  annual  charity  week,  which 
begins next week for Desert Angels.  There is a fundraiser 
at  Smash  Burger  this  Saturday  and  skating  at  the  Troy 
Sports  Center.   She  also  shared  that  second  semester  is 
upcoming standardized tests in March.   
Naven  Karoumy,  student  representative  from  Niles 
Community  High  School,  reported  to  the  Board  that  15 
students finished credits for high school diplomas and 12 
of them have already stated to attend college.  He shared 
requirements so far this school year and that three Niles 
alumni  recently  received  $1,500  scholarships  from  the

Negotiations Update 
Enrollment Projections 
It  was  moved  by  Mrs.  Edmunds  and  supported  by  Mr. 
Hauff that BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED that the Board of 
Education  takes  action  to
  approve  the  aforementioned 
Mr.  Witt,  Assistant  Superintendent,  Human  Resources, 
Finding will be held February 26 
– 28.   
Dr. Fowler presented a report on enrollment projections 
that  is  collected  and  analyzed  for
  projecting  enrollment 
in the District.     She talked about three projection levels 
in  the  report  –  low,  most  likely  and  high.   She  then 
explained  how  the  projections  are  calculated  for 
enrollment  using  those  variables.   She  also  mentioned 
that projected enrollment numbers become less reliable 
the  total  projected  enrollment  for  grades  K  –  12  for  the 
2014/2015  school  year.   She  completed  her  report  by 
revisiting  the  enrollment  numbers  that  had  been 
projected  for  2013/2014  at  this  time  last  year  and  then 
compared  them  to  the  actual  enrollment  numbers 
verified on official membership day.    
BUSINESS 
Best Practice Incentive 
WHEREAS,  Section  22f  of  the  State  School  Aid  Act 
provides  $52  per  pupil  one‐time  grants  to  districts  that 
June 1, 2014 (MCL 388.1622f); and; 
WHEREAS, the Board of Education of Troy School District 
desires  to  receive  the  $52  per  pupil  incentive  payment; 
and; 
WHEREAS, the Troy School District has satisfied at least 7 
of 8 best practices criteria; and; 
WHEREAS,  eligibility  for  the  incentive  payment  is 
contingent  upon  adopting  a  resolution  that  states  the 
District  has  complied  with  at  least  7  of  8  of  the  best 
practices criteria;  
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED AS FOLLOWS: 
A. The  Board  of  Education  of  Troy  School  District 
certifies  that  the  District  has  complied  with  the
following requirements: 
1) The  District  is  the  designated  policy  holder  for 
medical  benefit  plan(s)  pursuant  to  Section 
22f(1)(a).

Bid Package 9772  
Bid proposals were received from Waste Management of Michigan, Republic Service and 
Rizzo Environmental Services.  After reviewing the proposal and a fee analysis of each 
company, I am recommending that Rizzo Environmental Services be awarded the Container 
Refuse Collection Service for 2014-2015, 2015-2016 and 2016-2017 school years.   
Our current year estimated expense from Allied Waste is $77,000.00 based on the 2013-2014 
prices of $1.74 per cubic yard for trash and $1.66 per cubic yard for recycling.   
Our estimated future annual cost from Rizzo Environmental Services, based on the bid 
package of $1.48 per cubic yard for both trash and recycling, is: 
Elementary Schools   $      20,317.00 
Middle Schools   $      18,163.00 
High Schools   $      24,935.00 
Support Facilities   $        5,995.00 
  $      69,410.00 
If you have any questions, please do not hesitate in contacting my office.

6. C. PERSONNEL 
 CONSIDERATION:  NON-REPRESENTED EMPLOYEES 
It is recommended that the Board of Education approves a one -time, off-
schedule payment for all non -represented Troy School District (TSD) 
employees who regularly were em ployed by the District during the 
2012/2013 school year (with the exception of the Superintendent).   The 
amount of this one -time, off-schedule payment for the 2013/2014 school 
year shall equal four percent (4%) of each non -represented employee’s 
base wages /salary earned for the period from July 1, 2012 – June 30, 
2013.  This four percent (4%) amount is consistent with the amount of 
the one-time, off-schedule payment provided to TSD employees working 
in positions covered under the respective Collective Barga ining 
Agreements between the Troy School District and the Troy Educational 
Secretaries Association (TESA) and the Troy Educational Support 
Personnel Association for the 2013/2014 school year.   The base salaries 
for TSD employees under the applicable salary  schedules for non -
represented positions will remain frozen for the 2013/2014 school year.   
Likewise, step advancement on the applicable salary schedules for non -
represented positions will not be provided to any eligible non -represented 
employees for the 2013/2014 school year. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below: 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by          
 Supported by         
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the Board of Education takes action to approve 
the aforementioned personnel recommendation as stated above.

 7. A.   03.04.14 
7. CURRICULUM 
A. CONSIDERATION:   Schools Closed to Open Enrollment for  
        2014/2015 
Administration has reviewed the enrollment for elementary, middle, and high 
school attendance areas.  Due to the elementary building ’s total student 
enrollment and physical space constraints, we recommend that Bemis and 
Schroeder Elementary Schools be closed to open enrollment.  Our goal for 
Athens and Troy High has been to maximize each building’s enrollment which 
has resulted in  both schools being closed to open enrol lment for the past 
several years. Based on current enrollment projections f or the 201 4/2015 
school year, it is recommended that Athens and Troy High be closed to open 
enrollment with the exception of not more than 25  incoming ninth grade 
students who reside in the Troy High School attendance area, but wish to 
attend Athens High School .  Applications for the 25 spots at Athens High 
School will be accepted from March 5 - March 21, 2014. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Troy Board of Education adopts a resolution to 
close Bemis Elementary and Schroeder Elementary  School as well as  Athens High and 
Troy High School to open enrollment for the 201 4/2015 school year, with the exception 
of not more than 25 incoming ninth grade students who reside in the Troy High School 
attendance area , but wish to attend Athens High School.  Students with siblings 
attending Athens High School and currently enrolled in grades 9 – 11 will be allowed to 
open enroll to Athens for the 2014/2015 school year.

105
Windstream Primary Rate Interface (PRI – 
$4,361.40 
Blackboard (formerly 
Web Hosting $30,853.16 
Memorandum 
TO:  Dr. Fowler, Superintendent 
FROM: Mark Washington, Director of Technology 
RE: E-Rate/USF Bid Recommendations 
DATE: February 27, 2014 
Each year, the district submits a request through the Universal Services Fund Program allowing 
companies to submit competitive bids on telecommunications and internet services. Our E-
Rate/USF consultant Convergent Technology of Flint, Michigan, analyzes the submission and 
presents the district with a summary of those companies that chose to respond.  
Recommendation: 
For Primary Rate Interface (PRI-Phone Lines) a recommendation of continuing with Windstream 
is being submitted for an annual cost of $4361.40.  While they were not the low bidder, the cost 
to move the lines to another provider would be at least $1500 in fees and labor.  Windstream has 
provided the district solid performance and the savings is not enough reason to change 
companies.  
For Web Hosting, a recommendation to continue with Blackboard (formerly known as Edline) at 
an annual cost of $30853.16.  Blackboard currently provides an interface to the district’s grading 
program as well as the primary provider of our current website format.  While they are the 
second highest bidder, the cost of changing our grading system and all of the district public 
websites would be prohibitive.   
For Long Distance Service, a recommendation to continue with AT&T is being made as they 
were the only company to respond.  Their cost for service is $.0245/minute. 
For Cell Phone provider, Sprint is being recommended to continue the contract from the state bid 
for Smartphones at $49.99/month.  While Verizon has a lower monthly cost by $1.92/line per 
month, in order for the district to switch, we would have to break the current contract.  The early 
termination fees being billed by Sprint would exceed the value of the savings from the monthly 
charges.    
Attached please find detailed summary of the bid responses as received by Convergent 
Technology.   
Should you have any questions, please let me know if I can provide any further information.
